### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.50-159.00 
前週交易區間 
156.35 158.45

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1400 
前週交易區間 
1.1214 1.1391

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.29-115.10.02 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週為提存期底 ， 繼央行上週大砍存單效應持續發酵 ， 市
場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

市場焦點
美國
公債回購
• 美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，低於部分市場期待
的80億至100億美元規模 。民間持有美債規模達 31.8兆美元，相較之下 ，本次60億美元回購
僅約占民間持債規模 0.02%，對整體影響有限 。此外，約6成的美債期限不足 5年，意味未來
再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。
日本央行
• 日本第二季GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給
經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日本央行(BOJ)
逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶
值的政治壓力，彭博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。

美國
通膨
• 美國8月生產者物價指數 （PPI）年增5.4%，略高市場預期的 5.3%，核心PPI年增4.6%，符合
預期。8月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增3.35%、核心CPI年增2.4%，均符合市場預期，但核心
CPI月升幅0.3%，高於市場預期的 0.2%。數據公佈後，FedWatch顯示市場預期9月升息機率，
從前一周的50%攀升至86%。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不
均衡，能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數
據，9月升息1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而
是Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14

能源成本回升推升PPI，通膨壓力牽動Fed政策空間
• 能源成本回升，PPI重新升溫：美國8月PPI月增0.4%、年增5.4%，增幅均明顯高於7月，主要受到商品價格上漲帶動；其
中最終需求商品價格月增1.1%，能源價格上升4.2%，柴油更是大漲逾2成，成為商品價格上升的重要來源，惟最終需求服
務價格僅略增0.1%，顯示本次PPI升溫仍以能源及商品價格為主。
• 成本壓力仍在，利率政策不確定性升高： 扣除食品、能源及貿易服務後的核心 PPI月增0.3%，微低於7月的0.4%，但年增
4.7%，顯示價格壓力仍未完全消退；其中運輸與倉儲服務價格上升2.3%，反映部分成本仍具黏著性，致使市場對Fed利率
政策的重新定價明顯升溫，進而推動股、債市短線波動擴大。
• 市場觀察：由於本次PPI升溫主要來自能源成本 ，但服務價格壓力整體仍相對溫和 ，因此尚不足以視為通膨全面升溫 ，然

能源價格若持續走高 ，將提高Fed維持較高利率的壓力 ，建議投資人短線宜降低長天期債券與高估值成長股的追價 ，債券
可優先配置短中天期、高品質債券，股票則可關注能源、金融及具定價能力企業，提高投組對利率與成本波動的承受度。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，Producer Price Indexes – Aug,  2026，2026.9.11

• 財政部對美債的回購規模不如預期：美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，主要鎖定流

動性較低的舊券，以改善長天期債券市場流動性。雖然本次回購高於先前設定的每次至少40億美元，但低於部分市場期待的
80億至100億美元規模。消息公布後10年期公債殖利率逼近5%，30年期殖利率亦突破5.3%。
• 回購規模在高額赤字背景下，難以有效拉低長天期公債殖利率：美國今年公債發行規模較2025年增加11.8%，民間持有美債
規模達31.8兆美元、年增8.2%，相較之下，本次60億美元回購僅約占民間持債規模0.02%，對整體影響有限。此外，約6成
的美債期限不足5年，意味未來再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。反觀新興國家基本面
持續改善，且當地債市有貨幣潛在升值空間，可作為全球債券配置的分散來源。

全球主要市場及國家經濟成長率

• 地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性：美國和伊朗的衝突已經進入第7個月，能源價格上升及地緣政治風險仍
對全球經濟與市場信心形成壓力，但AI投資熱潮持續推升科技需求，使全球經濟展現優於預期的韌性。其中，亞洲仍是全球
成長動能最強的區域之一，2026年亞洲（不含日本）經濟成長率預估達4.7%，明顯高於全球平均2.9%及成熟市場1.6%。在
內需與科技出口支撐下，新興亞洲基本面相對穩健，具備較佳的中長期配置條件。

• AI資本支出延續，台韓成為核心受惠市場：AI資本支出持續擴張，2026年全球雲端AI資本支出預估達7,960億美元，並帶動
AI半導體市場至2030年突破7,500億美元。亞洲掌握全球半導體供應鏈核心，台灣受惠於先進製程與先進封裝需求，韓國則
受惠於HBM記憶體需求，台韓可望成為AI基礎建設擴張的主要受惠市場；同時，中國與印度則提供較穩定的內需及經濟成
長支撐。2026年亞洲企業盈餘成長率預估達72.13%，顯著高於全球平均，建議新興亞洲以台韓科技股為主要成長配置，並
搭配中國、印度市場分散區域風險。

短債供給增加未引發融資緊張，短天期債券防禦價值提升
• 回購市場維持穩定，短債供給暫未造成壓力：美國回購市場今年以來維持平穩，儘管9月短債償付與企業繳稅可能帶來短
暫資金波動，但SOFR與IORB利差仍處相對正常區間 ，顯示目前準備金與短期流動性尚稱充裕 。此外SOFR/IORB差值對
短期美債淨發行增加的敏感度明顯低於2025年底，反映流動性環境偏寬鬆。
• 貨幣基金成長放緩，供給吸收能力受到限制：零售資金流入偏弱，使貨幣市場基金資產增速落後 ，2026年迄今僅增加約
325億美元，遠低於2025年同期的2,050億美元；即便Q4季節性回升，新增資金對短債供給的承接能力仍可能有限。
• 後市觀察：年底前短債淨發行規模預估約3,250億美元，後續需關注短債供給、企業繳稅及財政部長端公債回購對資金面

的影響。建議投資人配置上可提高 1～3年期美國國庫券或短天期美債 ETF比重，以取得票息收益並降低長端殖利率波動
對債券價格的影響。
資料來源：US Treasury，FRBNY ，Federal Reserve ，2026.9.9

風險偏好升溫與債券供給增加，推升期限溢價與長債殖利率
• 全球期限溢酬上升，長債面臨重新定價：全球債券殖利率同步走高，不僅反映各國政策利率預期，也受到全球期限溢酬上升
推動；投資人對持有長天期政府債券要求更高補償 ，顯示長債殖利率上行已具有跨市場的結構性因素 。Bloomberg
Economics的模型亦顯示，全球期限溢酬是近年G7長債殖利率走高的重要共同因素。
• 風險資產吸引資金，放大長債供需壓力：AI投資題材推升股票市場、信用利差仍相對偏窄，加上大宗商品走強，使股票、公
司債及商品與政府債券形成資金競爭；在政府融資需求持續增加的情況下 ，若債券需求未同步擴張，政府公債需提供更高殖
利率才能吸引資金。
• 後市觀察：長端利率已成為股債配置的重要變數 ，若殖利率因財政供給與風險偏好維持高檔 ，長天期債券及高估值股票均可

能承壓；建議以短中天期美債及高品質投資級公司債為核心 ，待長端殖利率充分反映期限溢酬後，再分批拉長債券存續期間。
資料來源：Bloomberg Economics，2026.9.9

日本央行升息箭在弦上，但缺乏連續升息條件
• GDP修正支持升息 ，但關鍵點在配合日本和美國政府遏制日圓疲軟： 日本第二季 GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值
1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日
本央行(BOJ)逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶值的政治壓力 ，彭
博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。
• 缺乏連續大幅加息的支撐力道： (1)經濟衝擊風險：日圓升值效應已對經濟產生緊縮效果 ，彭博預估可能拖累實際 GDP成長
達0.3%。(2)財政負擔過重：若政策利率升至 1.75%以上，將大幅增加政府利息支出 ，擠壓高市內閣的財政刺激空間 。(3)企

業投資放緩：私人資本支出仍顯疲軟(季比下降0.9%)，顯示經濟復甦基礎尚未完全穩固。
• 小結：日本央行在政治、匯率與經濟基本面交集下 ，9月升息已成定局，但考量財政負擔與全球金融穩定後 ，其後續加息步
伐應偏向謹慎與循序漸進 ，密切關注9月日本央行會議後聲明 ，若央行強調循序漸進而非連續緊縮 ，市場波動相對可控；反
之，若釋放過度鷹派訊號，則需防範市場拋售風險。由於市場資金對日圓套利交易依賴度仍高，日圓任何短期內的急速升值，
都極易觸發全球風險資產的連鎖去槓桿效應。

• 8月份 CPI及PPI：消費者物 價指 數 (CPI)月增 0.4%(前月月減 0.1%)、年增 0.8%(前月 0.5%)，生 產者物價指數 (PPI) 月增

• 觀點：中國自2023年以來，內需市場面臨房地產崩盤 、青年失業率飆升以及公共債務攀升等困境 。今年年初以來，通膨雖
然由負轉正，不過從細項得知，因國際油價飆升、AI硬體設備面臨漲價壓力，本波通膨回升主要是受到供給端影響。外部成
本壓力恐進一步壓抑中國內需消費，中國要在真正意義上走出通縮環境可能還需要更多官方政策發力。彭博預估，在需求未
出現明顯改善的情境下，中國央行需要近一步放寬貨幣政策，預測年底前可能降息10個基點。

AI產業獲利亮眼，但需留意變現能力和資本效率
• AI獲利增速領先大盤，銷售成長成為獲利關鍵：AI板塊在2026年第一與第二季分別實現52.8%與59.7%的高獲利成長，受高
基期影響，未來四季度平均獲利成長預計放緩至 34.4%，但仍大幅優於非 AI板塊12.3%。預計2026年第三季至2027年第二
季，AI平均銷售成長率將達 27.2%，為非AI成分股(5%)5倍以上 。隨著未來利潤率擴張放緩 (預計未來 6個季度平均僅提升
1.66%，低於過去12個季度的3.52%)，持續的銷售成長是維持獲利的關鍵。
• AI供應鏈內部發展並不均衡： 半導體最為強勁，2026Q3至2027Q2預期銷售成長65.8%，獲利大增112.1%且淨利潤率大幅
擴張8.86%，為產業鏈核心引擎。雲端巨頭表現穩健，營收與獲利增速維持約26%，基礎設施軟體與硬體也有15%~20%營

收與獲利成長 。但應用端軟體成長相對乏力 ，未來四季度獲利預期增速僅 4.3%與6.5%，表現低於標普500非AI股平均水準。
• 資本強度創新高 ，但自由現金流承壓： AI資本支出佔營收比重自 2024年Q1的8.9%一路攀升至 2026年Q2的20.1%，預計
2026年下半年將達到23%的高點。資本支出激增導致自由現金流與資本支出之比(FCF/Capex)從2.48倍滑落至0.69倍，自由
現金流利潤率亦從21%降至13.9%。隨著現金逐漸消耗，企業可能需透過債券市場進行發債融資 ，以維持龐大基礎設施投資。
• 小結與投資建議：AI板塊依然是美股獲利驅動力，但隨利潤率提升空間受限、自由現金流受資本支出壓制，投資邏輯已從全
面暴漲轉向注重變現能力與資本效率。投資建議聚焦上游硬體與半導體，建議持續優選具備高定價能力與實質訂單支撐的半
導體與雲端基礎設施龍頭，留意資本支出佔比過高且自由現金流急劇惡化公司，優先選擇現金流充沛、融資成本低企業。
標普500指數盈利成長：AI vs. 非AI
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.7

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

## Document 160: AI“世纪IPO之战”：OpenAI奋起直追，与Anthropic差距正在缩小

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“世纪IPO之战”：OpenAI奋起直追，与Anthropic差距正在缩小.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

曾独占企业市场四分之三份额的 Anthropic ，正遭遇 OpenAI 的强⼒反扑。 GPT-5.6 系列以
激进降价撕开缺⼝， Fable 5 数据留存政策则加速了客⼾出⾛。两家万亿估值的 AI 巨头均在
筹备 IPO ，⽽企业客⼾ " 哪家便宜⽤哪家 " 的冷酷逻辑，正让这场价格战愈演愈烈。
摘要：
今 年 以 来 ， A I ⼤ 模 型 领 域 的 商 业 竞 争 格 局 正 在 发 ⽣ 显 著 逆 转 。 曾 ⼀ 度 主 导 企 业 市 场 的
Anthropic ，正⾯临 OpenAI 的强⼒追赶 —— ⽽这场⻆⼒的终极舞台，是两家公司即将到来的
IPO 。
据 OpenRouter 最新数据，在同时使⽤ Anthropic 和 OpenAI 产品的约 12 万家企业中，两家公司
的⽀出占⽐在 9 ⽉已⼤致持平。⽽在今年年初， Anthropic ⼀家独占这⼀群体总⽀出的四分之
三。
这⼀逆转的核⼼驱动⼒，是 OpenAI 今年夏季推出的 GPT-5.6 系列模型 —— 该系列以成本效率为

核⼼设计理念，并在发布后随即⼤幅降价。与此同时， Anthropic 在其旗舰模型 Fable 5 上设置的
数据留存政策，也令部分企业客⼾望⽽却步。
两家公司均⾯临资本⽀出急剧攀升的压⼒，且均在筹备 IPO ： Anthropic 最早可能于今年 11 ⽉上
市， OpenAI 则预计明年跟进。华尔街正密切审视两家公司能否以可持续的商业模式和营收增
⻓，⽀撑起各⾃逾万亿美元的估值。
GPT-5.6 ： OpenAI 的价格战反攻
OpenAI 的反攻，以今年 6 ⽉ GPT-5.6 系列的发布为标志性节点。该系列包含 Sol 、 Terra 和 Luna 三
款模型，覆盖不同能⼒层级，为企业客⼾提供了更灵活的成本选择。发布后不久， OpenAI 进⼀
步将 GPT-5.6 Luna 的价格下调 80% ， GPT-5.6 Terra 下调 20% 。
据华尔街⽇报报道，多位创始⼈和⾏业分析师将 GPT-5.6 的发布视为市场格局的转折点。
OpenRouter 洞察负责⼈ Peter Walker 表⽰： "GPT-5.6 为 OpenAI 开辟了⼀条新赛道，⽽

Anthropic 的模型系列⽬前并不具备这样的对应产品。 "
软件开发平台 Retool 的创始⼈兼 CEO David Hsu 表⽰，年初他的公司还在 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 之
间来回切换，但 GPT-5.6 的发布是 Retool 转向主要使⽤ OpenAI 模型的 " 主要催化剂 " 。 " 我们希望
找到最便宜的模型，因为模型越便宜，我们的收⼊就越⾼， "Hsu 说。他估计，⽬前使⽤ OpenAI
模型的成本⽐ Anthropic 低约 20% 。
Fable 5 数据政策： Anthropic 的隐患
OpenAI 的价格攻势之外， Anthropic ⾃⾝的政策失误也加速了部分客⼾的流失。 Anthropic 在今
年 6 ⽉初发布 Fable 5 时宣布，将出于信任与安全⽬的，默认保留⽤⼾数据 30 天。这⼀政策对涉
及敏感数据的⾏业构成合规障碍。
David Hsu 表⽰， Retool ⼏乎所有合同的默认条款都要求数据即时删除， Fable 5 的数据留存机

制使其⽆法满⾜这⼀要求，因此公司从未使⽤该模型。 Anthropic 此后尝试通过向部分客⼾提供
数据管控权限来弥补这⼀问题，但损失已经造成。
企业客⼾：成本压⼒重塑选择逻辑
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
在更宏观的层⾯，企业客⼾的决策逻辑正在发⽣结构性转变。随着越来越多员⼯尝试使⽤ AI ⼯
具，企业⾯临的成本压⼒持续上升，并逐渐意识到并⾮所有任务都需要最顶尖的模型。部分企
业开始将中国低成本开源模型纳⼊其多模型使⽤策略。
AI 销售平台初创公司 Reevo 的联合创始⼈兼 CEO David Zhu 表⽰，其公司正将 AI 使⽤从
Anthropic 转向 OpenAI ，原因有⼆：成本考量，以及降低对单⼀模型供应商的依赖。 " 与某⼀家
模型供应商深度绑定的蜜⽉期已经结束，"Zhu 说，但他也坦⾔偏好随时可能再度转变， " 事情变
化太快了。 "
⾦融科技公司 Ramp 的经济学家 Ara Kharazian 表⽰，他在 9 ⽉中旬观察了雇主旗下 7 万名客⼾的

数据，发现企业在 OpenAI 模型上的⽀出⾃去年 12 ⽉以来⾸次超过 Anthropic 。不过这⼀领先优势
转瞬即逝 —— 到当周末， Anthropic 以微弱优势重新夺回⾸位。
Anthropic ：市场热度犹存，新模型反击
尽管市场份额承压， Anthropic 在开发者社群中的号召⼒依然不容⼩觑。本周三上午，旧⾦⼭市
中⼼⼀处仓库外，⼤批⼈群排起⻓⻰，等待进⼊ Anthropic 主办的 "Claude Founder House" 活
动，部分⼈提前⼀⼩时到场。前⼀天，有⼈等候三⼩时后仍因场地爆满被拒之⻔外，现场盛况
在社交媒体上被⽐作科切拉⾳乐节的排队景象。
视频游戏初创公司 ReadyM 联合创始⼈ Michael Szklarski 表⽰，其公司通常需要最前沿的模型，
倾向于使⽤ Anthropic 的 Fable 5.1 ，但他希望与 Anthropic ⼯程师探讨如何进⼀步降低使⽤成
本。
在竞争压⼒下， Anthropic 于 9 ⽉下旬开始发布新⼀代 Claude 5.5 系列模型，主打更低成本与更⾼

效率。 AI 领域的领先优势向来转瞬即逝，这场争夺企业客⼾的价格战，远未到尘埃落定之时。

Key points
• The Cotality home value index –
covering all of Australia – fell 1.1% in
September following a downwardly
revised declines of 1.2% in August

and 1.4% in July. Annual growth
stalled flat with prices nationally now
5.2% below their peak in March.
• Declines continue to be widespread.
Across the major cities, prices fell
1.4% in Sydney, 1.5% in Brisbane,
1.3% in Adelaide, and 1.2% in Perth
with Melbourne recording a slightly
milder 0.7% decline. Sydney and
Melbourne continue to see more
pronounced declines with prices now
down 7%yr and 6.2%yr respectively.
• Preliminary estimates show the number

of sales, in rolling 3mth terms, down
3.9%qtr and 17.4%yr. The pace of
turnover falls has moderated from
the rapid 8.6%qtr recorded in July.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

BULLETIN
the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year
ago, albeit still not high compared to long run averages
(reflecting the extremely tight starting point for the Brisbane
market).
• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

also marked down from 0.8% to 1.1%, annual price growth
dropping to 6.5%yr. The price correction began later in
Adelaide but is now running at a similar pace to prices
nationally.
• Perth recorded a 1.2% decline but also saw big downward
revisions to previous months (August from 0.8% to –1.7%
and July from –1.3% to –1.8%). At 10.1%yr, annual growth
is still solidly positive but was tracking at over 25%yr earlier
in the year. As with Adelaide and Brisbane, the detail shows

a more even spread of declines across price tiers. However,
turnover is showing a milder pull-back in Adelaide.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.4%mth, 11.9%yr), relatively
resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in
Canberra (–1.1%mth, –1.6%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

## Document 202: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-05)

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 209: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-09).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

## Document 262: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

The bank
for a changing
world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Share markets generally stalled across the globe. In the US,
stocks partially retraced gains from earlier in the week, with
the S&P 500 (–0.2%), Dow Jones (–0.7%) and NASDAQ
(–0.2%) all declining. Stocks fared worse across the pond,

with the Euro Stoxx 50 effectively unwinding all gains in
recent days (–1.5%), marked by chunky declines in Germany’s
DAX (–1.4%) and France’s CAC (–1.2%), alongside a larger
decline in London’s FTSE 100 (–0.8%). Stocks were also
weaker across much of Asia, falling in Tokyo (–0.9%), Hong
Kong (–0.6%) and Seoul (–2.0%). The ASX 200 only edged
slightly lower, down –0.1%, but futures markets are pointing
to a weaker open this morning.
•  The treasury yield curve steepened overnight. Gains in the

shorter-end saw 2-year yields fall 3bps to 4.77% – this was
despite a strong lift in consumer inflation expectations and
a hawkish FOMC Minutes, with market pricing for further
rate hikes from the FOMC instead softening at the margin.
Meanwhile, elevated yields at the long end drew very strong
demand at the latest 10-year auction, stabilising 10-year
yields at around 5.28%.
•  Bonds were more varied elsewhere, with Bunds bull
steepening amid a steady long-end, while Gilts bear

steepened amid a 6bp lift in the 10-year. ACGBs were
broadly steady during local trading, with yields inching only
slightly higher, though that gave way to gains in futures
trading overnight. Swaps markets continue to have around
6-7bps priced in for the RBA’s November meeting, while a
rate hike in December is seen at closer to 50/50 odds.
•  The USD bounced back overnight as the DXY rallied to an
intraday high of 102.5 before settling 0.4% higher at around

102.3. The Euro led the way lower, falling 0.6% versus the
greenback to just under USD1.12, back around its recent
lows. The Sterling shared a similar fate, down 0.5% toward
the USD1.321 level, remaining largely rangebound between
USD1.32-1.33 since late September. The Yen bucked the
wider trend, holding broadly steady. The Aussie dollar
meanwhile fell 0.3% to around USD0.696, unwinding gains
from earlier in the week.
•  Crude oil prices remain elevated as markets try to evaluate

the net impact of opposing forces – signs of recovering flows
of oil out of the Middle East is a positive, but reports that
Iran has increased the number of attacks on ships transiting
the Strait of Hormuz has kept a floor under prices. Brent was
up 0.5% to US$101.1/bbl overnight, still unable to break
sustainably under the US$100/bbl mark as of yet. Copper
prices lifted 0.4% to US$14,476 on the LME, gains being
driven by risks of tighter supply amid strikes at a major

Chilean mine, though the turn in risk sentiment overnight and
firmer USD capped gains. Gold prices meanwhile fell 1.3% to
around US$4,111/oz.
International Data
In the US, the FOMC’s Minutes  from the September meeting
reinforced the hawkish sentiment present at the time of the
decision to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a midpoint
of 3.875%. “All” participants supported the decision to raise
rates in September, and “most” saw further tightening by

year-end as appropriate. Underscoring that view was the fact
that “almost all” participants saw inflation risks as tilted to the
upside. Importantly, the assessment of the inflation outlook at
the time had already anticipated a 0.2ppt downward revision
to PCE inflation from the BEA’s change in methodology, which
ended up being 0.3ppt in the event.
The NY Fed’s measure of consumer inflation expectations
for the year ahead meanwhile surprised to the upside, rising

from 3.58% in for to 3.90% in September, marking the highest
result so far this year. Expectations also nudged 0.1ppt higher
to 3.3% at the three-year horizon, though they were stable at
3.0% at the five-year horizon.
Local Data
The Westpac Economics New Zealand team published its
September Retail Spending Pulse , which found that retail
spending remained resilient in the face of hgher fuel prices
(see here).

US Treasuries have calmed. But plenty of opportunity and time
for markets to (re-)change their tune. Italian bond investors

potentially improve governability. We draw the parallel with
France, where improved governability is a prerequisite for a

Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Benjamin Schroeder
With the UST 10yr yield dipping below 5.25%, it does not mean the bond
market sell-oﬀ is over, but rather on pause
Treasuries sail calmer waters, but we'd not
get too comfortable
It's been a good week for US bond auctions. Solid 10yr and 30yr
auctions over consecutive days show there is a level where buyers
step in. The 30yr auction saw a strong international and domestic
bid that took down the paper at secondary market levels, and the
previous day's 10yr auction came a tad rich to secondary. The 10yr
yield is back below 5.25%, partly a reﬂection of this, but also
reﬂective of some value hunters generally. It does not mean the

bond market sell-oﬀ is over, but it does at least put it on pause, at

We also note that Treasuries are holding on to the moderate
richness built since the increased long-end-buyback
announcement, in the guise of tighter swap spreads; the antithesis
of what we saw in France over the past weeks, where swap spreads
gapped wider by a factor of at least two. Nothing like that has
occurred in the US, as the 10yr yield holds at just under a 40bp

spread to 10yr SOFR.
That said, the 10yr swap spread is oﬀ its recent lows, and we
continue to anticipate a likely widening in US swap spreads ahead
as we head into the mid-terms with not nearly enough chat on
ﬁscal deﬁcit-reduction actions plans. Plenty of opportunity for
markets to wake up and decide to start to price that through a
widening in the implied Treasury credit spread to the SOFR risk-free
rate.
Italy highlights the importance of
governability

Thursday saw a recovery in European government bond spreads
that was led by Italian bonds. Their 10y spread over Bunds
tightened around 5bp towards 110bp. This is still above the levels
seen before French repricing started to spill over, but it came with a
backdrop of rising energy prices. Since February, higher oil prices
have tended to generally widen spreads and in particular hurt
Italian bonds relatively more.
The outperformance was attributed to the Italian PM Meloni

securing the ﬁnal vote for her electoral reform that would grant any
leading coalition with more than 42% of the votes bonus seats to
secure stable majorities. It is actually unclear whether she will
beneﬁt from this, but the prospect of clearer majorities and better
governability is in general a positive for anyone looking for
exposure to sovereign risk.
It also highlights what we see as a precondition for any lasting
recovery in French bond spreads – political predictability and

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.
In the spotlight for October 2026: US-Iran war and EU-China relations

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank

Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.

The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.

Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,
Lebanon and Iran. This is the first Knesset since 1988 to actually complete a full four-year term without
early dissolution. Benjamim Netanyahu remains the dominant single figure but faces a fragmented field:
Naftali Bennett’s new “Together” party is polling as the main rival bloc, alongside Gadi Eisenkot’s Yashar,

Benny Gantz’s Blue and White, and the further right Ben Gvir and Smotrich factions.
Israel’s proportional system means the election is effectively about coalition formation. Key faultlines to
watch: the ultra-Orthodox draft-exemption fight, how the war’s legacy and hostage/reconstruction issues
in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

The foreign ministers’ meeting comes ahead of the December Leaders’ Summit in Miami under the US’
chairmanship of the G20 this year. Trump’s G20 year has organized around four expert tracks – trade,

It will be a test of whether the G20 can sustain cooperation amid intensifying geopolitical fragmentation.
In addition, Russia has confirmed its intent to actively participate in the meeting. This would further
normalize Russia’s multilateral engagement. For Europe and other partners, a key question will be the
extent to which the G20 can be a forum for broader international cooperation.

資產 4Q26 看法 投資展望
核心消費 中立 從已公告的核心消費個股展望，本行預期今年上下半年營收成長48：52
的溫和成長預期，同時零售商備貨行為與終端消費需求仍支持到貨量的
基本盤，但消費者實際支出意願可能受到油價／利率上升抑制，導致平
均單價、毛利率受到一定壓縮，尤其在低、中所得族群及易受油價影響
的旅遊／實體零售類別。4Q26預計呈「量能可望維持、獲利偏弱」格局
資料來源：美國銀行2026/9/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/18。

消費產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/21
本行長期追蹤美國銀行信用卡消費統計數據(Consumer
checkpoimt，月報) 顯示，8月消費動能自7月疲弱中明顯
反彈。從美國銀行發行的信用卡、簽帳卡合計消費YoY成
長率已連續三個月降溫（6月6.3%→7月5.0%→8月4.5%）
剔除油品後同步走緩（5.6%→4.3%→3.7%），主因6月世
界盃與線上促銷時序錯位帶來的高基期效應消退。惟季調
後MoM動能出現反轉，7月一度下滑（-0.2%），8月明顯
回升（+0.9%）。商務部數據同步佐證：8月零售銷售季調
後月增1.2%，優於市場預期0.7%，成長動能來自線上零售、
加油站（價格推升）及餐飲、電子產品等類別。

《「K型」分化持續收斂，低所得消費並非來自舉債》
8 月非必需消費 YoY成長 ， 高所得 （ 5.9% ） 與低所得
（5.7%）差距僅0.2個百分點 ，為2024年1月以來最小；
中所得 （5.1%）略為落後 。低所得族群稅後工資成長已
連續第二個月領先其他所得層。
另紐約聯準銀行資料顯示 ，2026年第二季家庭總負債為

18.771兆美元 ，季減130億美元 ，年增3,830億美元；信
用卡餘額季增210億美元、年增540億美元至1.263兆美元，
負債溫和成長，並非急遽加槓桿。嚴重違約（90天以上）
的 年 化 轉 出 率 方 面， 信 用 卡 由 去 年 同 期6.93% 微升至
6.97%。這與美銀 Checkpoint 揭露的另一項正面訊號互
相呼應：所有所得層級「每月全額繳清信用卡帳單」的家
庭比例都在上升，僅繳最低應繳金額的比例雖也上升但幅
度較小。綜合來看，原先市場擔憂的低所得者會藉由舉債
消費，但目前數據並不支持這樣的看法 ，等於是提供K型
收斂假說的有力證據
《重塑消費環境與消費類股估值基礎》
9月上旬發生兩項關鍵變化 ，根本性重塑消費環境與消費
類股估值基礎：聯準會於 9/16啟動三年多來首次升息 （1
碼至3.75%-4.00%），新任主席沃許（Kevin Warsh）強
調通膨仍偏高 ，點陣顯示年內可能再加 1碼；同時密西根
大學消費者信心指數9月初值急跌至47.8，創自1978年以

來次低。
資料來源：美國銀行 2026/9/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/18

《4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up需求強弱度分析》

由於NRF尚未發布2026年假期旺季（11-12月）專屬銷售
預測（歷年慣例於10-11月發布），且目前可用最新官方
預測為發生在中東戰爭之前。因此改採bottom-up方式，
以供需鏈前置指標、零售商財測、消費者信心與成本端壓
力四大面向交叉驗證。

律，顯示零售商對4Q旺季需求仍持樂觀，提前備貨以
應對可能的關稅／運輸不確定性。

+10%），反映消費者尋求高性價比商品的行為持續強
化。
➢ 關稅退款效應將於Q3起逐漸消退，市場焦點將轉向排
除一次性利益後的核心毛利率與同店銷售動能是否可持
續。
➢ 燃油與運費成本為共同逆風：各公司財報及法說會均提
及油價、運輸成本上升對下半年毛利率的壓力。
結論：4Q26傳統旺季需求呈現「量能可望維持、獲利
品質面臨分化壓力」的中性格局，與兩個月前「消費持續
熱絡」的單一樂觀敘事相比，風險報酬比已明顯向下傾斜
(亦即產業獲利成長動能趨緩)，整體消費的評價面難有向
上提升的動力來源。

### Page 5

### Page 4

3
中国
◼ 製造業PMIは拡大ペースが加速
８月のRatingDog製造業PMIは51.5と、
前月（50.9）から拡大ペースが加速。世
界的なハイテク・AIインフラ需要を背景
に電子機器などの新規受注が堅調に推移
した。
◼ インフレ率は小幅に加速
８月の総合CPIは前年比＋0.8%と、前月
（同＋0.5%）から小幅に加速したもの
の依然として低水準で推移。非食品分野
は同＋1.2%上昇した一方、豚肉価格が
同▲11.8%と下落して食品全体のデフレ
圧力を強め、総合CPIの上昇を抑えた。
◼ 消費は低位で推移
８月の小売売上高は前年比＋0.4%にと
どまり、消費の回復ペースが鈍化。８月
の不動産販売面積は同▲13.0％と、依然
として減少が続いており、不動産市況の
低迷が長期化している。政府はリスク抑
制のため未完成物件の事前販売規制を強
化を打ち出している。
◼ 輸出は増加幅が拡大
８月の輸出は前年比+25.0%と、前月
（同+23.9%）から増加幅が拡大。世界
的なAI投資の拡大を背景に、半導体など

のハイテク製品や機械電気製品の出荷が
増加した。
輸出消費

（注）直近は7日物リバースレポが９/23日、その他が８月。

### Page 5

4
◼ PMIは引き続き拡大
９月の総合PMIは56.5と、前月
（54.3）から拡大ペースが加速。製造
業PMI、サービス業PMIともに加速し、
ヘッドラインを押し上げた。
◼ インフレ率は加速
８月の総合CPIは前年比＋4.8%と、前
月（＋4.4%）から加速。モンスーン期
の天候不順による一部野菜、生鮮食品
の価格高騰に加え、国際原油価格の底
堅さが輸送コストを押し上げた。
◼ 自動車販売は引き続き増加
８月の自動車販売台数は前年比＋
17.5％と、前月（同＋25.9％）に続き
増加。昨年９月からのGST（物品サー
ビス税）引き下げにより、二輪車や乗
用車、商用車など幅広い車種で販売が
増加した一方、モンスーンの降雨不足
によりトラクターの販売は鈍化した。
◼ 鉱工業生産は引き続き増加
７月の鉱工業生産指数は前年比＋6.7%
と、前月（＋8.8%）から拡大ペースは
鈍化したものの高水準を維持。鉱業部
門の縮小が下押し要因となったが、製
造業および電力部門の力強い稼働が全
体を支えた。

◼ PMIは拡大ペースがわずかに鈍化
８月の製造業PMIは52.3と、前月
（53.1）からわずかに鈍化したものの、
景気拡大の節目である50を引き続き上
回った。
◼ BOKはインフレ抑制の利上げ
８月の総合CPIは前年比＋3.1 %と、前
月（同＋2.8%）から再加速。前年８月
に、通信大手が顧客情報流出への補償対
応として携帯電話料金を50%減免して
いた裏要因で、情報通信の寄与度が拡大、
総合CPIを押し上げた。
８月27日、韓国銀行（BOK）は利上げ
を決定（＋25bps、2.75％→3.00％）。
利上げの背景については、声明文で「イ
ンフレ圧力の拡大を先制的に阻止すると
ともに、金融安定リスクへの警戒を継続
する必要がある」とした。
◼ 消費は前月比で減少
７月の小売売上高は前期比▲2.4％と、
前月（同＋2.7％）から減少に転じた。
◼ 輸出は引き続き増加
８月の輸出は前年比＋68.7%と引き続
き増加。世界的なAI設備投資の拡大によ
り、半導体輸出が同＋209.1 %と驚異的

な伸びを示し、自動車の一時的落ち込み
（同▲29.8%）を完全に相殺した。

◼ PMIは縮小に転じる
８月の総合PMIは49.5と、前月
（51.0）から悪化し、縮小に転じた。
新規受注の伸び悩みや、中東情勢によ
る国際物流コストの上昇が民間企業の
景況感を圧迫した。
◼ HKMAはFRBに追随して利上げ
８月の総合CPIは前年比＋1.7％と、前
月（同＋1.7％）から横ばいで推移。
９月17日、香港金融管理局（HKMA）
は利上げを決定（＋25bps、4.00％
→4.25％）。前日にFRBが利上げを決
定したことを受けて、これに追随した。
◼ 消費は緩やかに増加
７月の実質小売売上高は前年比＋
2.4％と、前月（同＋2.2％）に続き増
加。週末を中心に香港住民が深センな
ど中国本土へ越境して買い物を行う
「北上消費」により、域内の百貨店や
外食チェーンの売上が伸び悩んだ。
◼ 輸出は引き続き増加
７月の輸出量は前年比＋31.7％と、前
月（同＋34.2％）に続き増加。AI関連
を中心とする域内貿易の活発化により、
輸出量が増加した。

## Document 465: 中东冲突再升级：沙特机场遇袭、波斯湾油轮中弹，特朗普拟对伊朗再动手，布油站上102美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/中东冲突再升级：沙特机场遇袭、波斯湾油轮中弹，特朗普拟对伊朗再动手，布油站上102美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

胡塞武装连续袭击利雅得和艾⼘哈两座机场，造成 3 死 36 伤；波斯湾深处油轮遭弹射物击
中，为近⼀个⽉来⾸次。⽩宫已要求五⻆⼤楼制定选前对伊打击⽅案，彻底颠覆市场此
前 " 特朗普选前克制 " 的主流预期。双海峡联动⻛险加剧，叠加供应端的扰动因素持续叠加，
油价⼤幅攀升。
摘要：
中东局势在数⽇内急剧恶化。胡塞武装连续袭击沙特两座主要机场，造成⼈员死伤；⼀艘油轮
在卡塔尔附近海域遭弹射物击中，为近⼀个⽉来波斯湾深处⾸次遭袭；与此同时，⽩宫据报已
要求五⻆⼤楼制定可在美国中期选举前执⾏的对伊朗打击⽅案。多重冲击叠加之下，布伦特原
油价格突破 102 美元 / 桶，市场对中东供应中断的担忧骤然升温。
据央视新闻报道，也⻔冲突近⽇显著升温。胡塞武装军事发⾔⼈叶海亚 · 萨雷亚 7 ⽇发表声明称，
过去 24 ⼩时内，沙特对也⻔ 6 个省份发动了 156 次空袭和导弹袭击，本轮局势升级以来沙特实施
的空袭和导弹袭击总数已达 1798 次。与此同时，胡塞武装以弹道导弹和⽆⼈机对沙特境内多处
⽬标发动反击，袭击范围直抵⾸都利雅得。

华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道，据彭博报道，《⼤西洋⽉刊》援引两名美国政府官员披露，⽩宫已要求五
⻆⼤楼制定针对伊朗⽬标的打击⽅案，相关⾏动可能在下⽉中期选举前付诸实施。这⼀消息直
接打破了市场此前的主流预期 —— 即特朗普将在选前维持相对克制态势。
除了地缘紧张局势升级影响油价之外，供应端的扰动因素持续叠加。据华尔街⻅闻⽂章提及，
热带⻛暴 Isaias 导致墨西哥湾超过四分之⼀的原油产量被迫关停，据海洋矿产管理局数据，该⻛
暴预计将在未来数⽇增强为强⼆级飓⻛。与此同时，美国官⽅数据显⽰，上周全国原油及燃料
出⼝回升⾄季节性历史⾼位，全国原油库存下降 320 万桶。
沙特双机场遭袭， 3 死 36 伤
据彭博报道，伊朗⽀持的胡塞武装对沙特两座主要机场发动袭击，造成重⼤⼈员伤亡。
沙特⺠航总局（ GACA ）在声明中确认，位于⾸都利雅得的哈⽴德国王国际机场和西南部的艾⼘
哈国际机场于 6 ⽇⾄ 7 ⽇先后遭到袭击，共造成 3 ⼈死亡、 36 ⼈受伤。其中，利雅得机场袭击造成
1 名苏丹籍男性死亡，另有 5 名沙特公⺠、 2 名埃及⼈及 1 名巴基斯坦居⺠受伤；艾⼘哈机场袭击

造成 1 名摩洛哥籍⼥性和 1 名阿尔及利亚籍⼥性死亡， 28 ⼈受伤，伤情轻重不⼀。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
据央视新闻，胡塞武装发⾔⼈萨雷亚称，此次袭击动⽤了弹道导弹和⽆⼈机，打击⽬标还包括
海⽶斯穆谢特空军基地、阿基法军营及吉赞地区两处军营，造成两座机场空中交通受到影响，
军事基地受损，数⼗名军事⼈员伤亡。
沙特主导的多国联军随即展开⼤规模报复⾏动。联军发⾔⼈ Turki Al-Malki 在声明中宣布，联军
摧毁了胡塞武装位于也⻔萨达、荷台达、焦夫和⻢⾥⼘四省的82 处军事⽬标，包括弹道导弹储
存地点、指挥控制中⼼及通信系统。
Al-Malki 表⽰，胡塞武装袭击沙特境内平⺠和⺠⽤设施的⾏为 " 必将受到惩罚 " 。联军还称，已拦
截⼀枚从也⻔⾸都萨那发射、⻜向利雅得以北的弹道导弹，并摧毁了发射平台。
波斯湾深处油轮中弹，近⼀个⽉来⾸次
据彭博报道，英国皇家海军下属机构英国海上贸易⾏动处（ UKMTO ）在周三晚间的报告中确

认，⼀艘油轮在卡塔尔⻢迪纳特阿什沙⻢勒以北海域遭弹射物击中，造成⼈员伤亡，当局正在
展开调查。 UKMTO 未披露船只名称及袭击⽅⾝份。
据彭博看到的客⼾简报，安全顾问已将该船确认为⼀艘悬挂安提⽠和巴布达旗帜的⼩型油轮，
运载燃料或化学品。
这是约⼀个⽉以来波斯湾深处⾸次发⽣油轮遭袭事件。上⼀次类似事件发⽣在 9 ⽉ 9 ⽇，当时⼀
艘船只在伊拉克法奥东南约 28 海⾥处被不明弹射物击中。此后的袭击事件⼤多集中在霍尔⽊兹
海峡及其周边⽔域。
据彭博报道，伊朗已加快在霍尔⽊兹海峡的袭击频率， UKMTO 本⽉迄今已记录该⽔道发⽣ 9 起
袭击事件，约为 9 ⽉全⽉报告数量的⼀半，且与过境油轮流量回升的时间节点⾼度吻合。
特朗普拟选前对伊动⼿，市场预期被颠覆
据彭博报道，《⼤西洋⽉刊》援引两名美国政府官员披露，⽩宫已要求五⻆⼤楼制定可在中期选
举前执⾏的对伊朗打击⽅案，相关规划由负责协调战区⾏动的美国中央司令部负责推进。
据新华社报道，特朗普 6 ⽇在⻢⾥兰州斯帕罗斯⻆发表讲话时称，美国对伊朗的军事⾏动 " 做得很

棒 " ，并明确表⽰⾏动 "必须收尾了" 。他同时指出， " 唯⼀要考虑的问题是以哪种⽅式收尾，是以
柔和的⽅式或是强硬的⽅式 " ，措辞留有明显的战略模糊空间。
据彭博援引《⼤西洋⽉刊》报道，⽀持打击⽅案的官员认为，此举可令特朗普在选前展⽰强硬
姿态，并为共和党争取政治动能。然⽽，即便是⽅案的⽀持者也承认，有限度的打击⾏动本⾝
并不⾜以迫使伊朗回到谈判桌、恢复霍尔⽊兹海峡的安全通⾏，或在选举⽇前拉低油价。报道
还指出，打击规模、⽬标选择及是否最终付诸实施，⽬前仍在讨论之中，⼀种可能的路径是选
前先实施有限打击，选后再跟进更⼤规模⾏动。
这⼀报道与市场此前的主流判断形成直接⽭盾 —— 外界普遍认为特朗普不会在选前主动升级冲
突，以免令选⺠对这场已拖累共和党候选⼈的战争产⽣更多负⾯联想。

核心观点
请务必阅读最后重要事项
• 外需以及外需相关的供给强势不改，内需相关行业弱势且边际改善不多。我们认为，库存周期或仍以主动加为主，且价格对名义增速上
行支持力度暂时仍在高位，有利于名义增长的同比读数。而内需对供给的带动仍待观察。目前情况之下，政策加码概率上升，后期焦点
位于海外，关注地缘风险等是否持续“高烧不退”。内需方面，9月将是最后的验证阶段。
• 此前二季度再通胀加速受到输入性因素影响，且目前来看该因素短期有重新上升态势，即使9月油价冲高回落，也难改商品重心上移趋势，
是平减指数维持高位的重要因素。同时，中下游AI资本品涨价对总体通胀影响程度上升。上述两项逻辑将是通胀的主要影响。我们看到，
更多数据显示需求-价格正反馈力度增加，经济周期、产业周期、供需节奏仍有利于价格走升，新涨价动能或维持强势，再通胀步伐放慢
幅度有限。未来对名义增长预计不会产生拖累。
• 政策方面，今年上半年经济总体改善，名义增速明显加快，但K型分化显著。该情况延续至三季度，虽然外需维持强势，但财政-内需链

边际走弱的短板仍在。存量流动性对大类资产影响仍不一，也与风险偏好有关。往后看，年内名义增速或基本持稳，社融增速低于预期
后关注政策变动，暂时认为央行降息降准动力不强。
• 上半年财政支出先加速后放慢，广义财政对经济的支持作用逐步减弱，三季度仍呈现此特征。往后来看，狭义财政内生性修复出现后，
持续动能不足，短期拖累支出节奏。但政府融资增量已经回升，资金总量相对确定情况下，未来资金投放落地节奏仍是重点关注方向。
后续可关注名义增速再加快的动能，但土地相关收入对地方政府支出能力的约束暂时不变，是广义财政增长的主要不确定性。我们预计，
新一轮财政扩张仍在路上，后期对经济的带动力将会回升。
• 对于商品市场，外部地缘冲击带来的不确定性加大、美联储加息预期明显上升（以及最终加息落地）是9月影响商品市场的主要因素。但
目前来看，两者均呈现利空出尽的特征，对价格拖累明显下降，有利于市场整体反弹。长期来看，全球经济周期转向、美联储和其他主
要央行政策、资源民族主义等因素仍是商品价格的主要扰动，地缘政治风险、资本开支约束、厄尔尼诺风险将对能化、有色、农产品产

生持续影响。
资料来源：Wind、同花顺、方正中期期货研究院

经济基本面与宏观供需第一部分
货币与财政政策第三部分
价格水平变动情况第二部分
汇率与商品市场波动第四部分

### Page 4

4
8月数据显示，产出明显改善，投资、消费延续弱势，内需改善有限，新旧经济的K型分化仍在加大。
需求方面看。8月基建开始企稳，且下半年基建基数整体偏低，同比读数进一步下行空间有限。但具体节奏仍需关注资金投放情况。我们预
计，基建对总投资和经济拖累将会减小。长期看，新基建仍存在较大空间，前期政策所强调的“六网”建设将持续发力，但部分“新基建”
不会体现在传统基建口径中。
货币政策稳定，融资成本低位，制造业投资受经济转型、产业调整和升级影响，长期逻辑不变。外需对制造业投资存在较强拉动，新基建
投资部分位于制造业投资口径下，均是制造业投资维持强势的有利因素。同时，需求尤其是外需明显改善对制造业信心带动增强。8月制造
业走弱或仍是阶段性资本开支放缓的不利作用。未来需关注海外政策阶段性收紧带来的负面影响。
地产方面，近两年地产行业资金链自下而上走弱逻辑仍不变。上半年中游资金面收紧力度、持续性不强，未能带动投资端。政策层面仍是
局部、定向调整为主，系统性政策不多。央行放松住房公积金贷款时间限制，但预计影响有限。我们认为，仍需继续关注市场信心能否修

复、房价下行螺旋能否改善。行业阶段性底部位置、节奏仍待确认。
消费依然维持低位附近。服务消费强于商品消费的结构性特征不变，暑期消费或存在结构性、阶段性影响。后期商品消费补贴导致的高基
数因素逐步减弱，消费增速下行空间相对有限，但向上增量仍有待确认。此外，需警惕油价再度上涨造成的收入效应的负面影响。
总体上看，外需以及外需相关的供给强势不改，内需相关行业弱势且边际改善不多。我们认为，库存周期或仍以主动加为主，且价格对名
义增速上行支持力度暂时仍在高位，有利于名义增长的同比读数。而内需对供给的带动仍待观察。目前情况之下，政策加码概率上升，后
期焦点位于海外，关注地缘风险等是否持续“高烧不退”。内需方面，9月将是最后的验证阶段。
基本面与宏观供需——主要观点

### Page 5

5
年内经济增长逻辑或将略有变化
• 预计2026年下半年与上半年相比，经济增长逻辑改变，价格对名义增长带动仍在，同时增量逻辑强化，但K型分化则依然显著。
• 8月情况显示，中国经济供给升、需求缓、价格涨、库存降。需求端外需明显好于内需状况不变。库存周期回到主动加阶段。
• 通缩结束但再通胀放慢。一季度平减指数回升至0附近，二季度大幅上升至1.59%，三季度仍有上升空间，四季度或转向稳定。
• 数据来源：Wind、ifind，方正中期期货研究院整理
